市場焦點
美國
• 9月CPI年增3.7%、PPI年增6.3%，雙雙高於前期 ，能源價格的急升是通膨回升的主要推力 。
雖然核心CPI年增率僅升至2.6%，但住房與服務價格仍維持黏著，使通膨降溫過程出現反覆；
同時PPI增幅明顯高於 CPI，反映企業成本壓力偏高 ，未來不排除進一步向終端價格傳導 。整
體而言，CPI與PPI同步回升反映通膨降溫進程仍存在不確定性 ，進而令市場重估通膨回落速
度與對Fed貨幣政策路徑的審慎預期。
AI
• AI雲端業者 CoreWeave的5年期CDS利差一度突破 1,140個基點 ，旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近
15%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延至股票市場 。全球最大半導體設備商ASML表示，客戶已開始洽
談2029至2030年的訂單；晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期 。與此同時，台灣出口增

速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季
相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。
中國
• 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力
仍在，彭博表示以GDP平減指數衡量 ，經歷三年下滑後 ，整體經濟預計將在 2026年走出通縮 ，
投資上短期可聚焦，受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。

美股獲利引擎轉向半導體/核心AI與能源
• 雖然標普500指數在2026年第三季展現出獲利韌性 ，但隨著油價上漲侵蝕利潤率 、消費者受通膨壓抑 ，以及分析師下調未
來預測動能，市場未來的容錯空間正在縮小。
• AI與半導體仍為獲利領頭羊：科技與半導體持續為標普500指數獲利成長的核心動力，其中約20家企業組成的核心AI一籃子
股票(權重約34.6%)對指數獲利的貢獻度已超過科技七雄(Mag 7)，且此優勢預計將至少延續至2028年底。
• 油價推升能源產業，接棒成為強棒：受到近期國際油價上揚影響，能源產業獲利預測顯著上修，預計獲利成長勁道將延續至
2027年初。在接下來的三個季度中，能源板塊的收益成長表現預計將超越科技七雄。
• 消費與運輸業面臨成本壓頂：相較於科技與能源的強勁，必需消費品與交通運輸業正承受顯著壓力。例如，達美航空受高燃

料成本拖累，而Nike與百事可樂則反映出消費者因持續通膨而縮減支出的困境。
7月以來EPS和營收成長預期修正
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
剔除能源、科技七雄和AI一籃子股票後的指數收益成長

利潤率高檔盤整、關稅退款帶來短補，但動能已見放緩
• 利潤率高檔盤整，淨利潤率提升受限：標普500成分股第二季營運利潤率(18.7%)與淨利潤率(15.6%)均創下新高。雖然第三
季營運利潤率預估可進一步提升至19.4%，但淨利潤率預計微幅上升至15.8%，顯示整體企業淨利潤的擴張動能正漸趨平緩 。
• 700億美元關稅退款提供短線補藥： 受惠於美國政府依據國際緊急經濟權力法 (IEEPA)的追償退稅，估計仍有至少700億美元
的關稅款將退還給標普 500成分股(主要集中於工業、非必需消費及醫療保健等 )，這預計能為2026年標普500指數每股收益
(EPS)成長帶來約235個基點、利潤率提升約34個基點的短期提振。
• 上修動能開始轉弱：雖然整體財報指引動量仍維持正值，但過去六週內，分析師對未來12個月EPS與收入的修正動能出現頻

繁下修，這意味著市場先前的極度樂觀情緒正在消退，未來財報季度要優於預期的門檻正在提高。
• 小結與投資建議： 標普500指數短期內靠著半導體 /AI與能源雙引擎拉抬 ，加上關稅退款的一次性利多注入 ，下半年整體獲
利依然具備支撐。然而，高油價帶來的營運成本上升 ，以及終端消費力道的疲軟 ，正逐漸侵蝕非科技/非能源企業的利潤 ，
而大盤廣度不足(僅少數板塊支撐獲利)，代表未來投資風險正逐漸集中 ，但AI算力需求仍屬硬性需求，但波動加劇下，投資
建議以定期定額方式投資美股核心AI與半導體龍頭股票。
利潤率預測
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
各產業第二季度收到的關稅退款
未來12月EPS預期修正動能與營收

顧
美國9月CPI、PPI 同步升溫，能源衝擊擴大通膨與利率風險
• 能源價格急升，消費通膨再升溫：美國9月CPI月增0.6%、
年增3.7%，高於前月 3.4%，其中汽油月增 9.1%、能源
價格月增 5.2%，汽油單項即貢獻整體 CPI當月漲幅逾半；
另一方面，核心CPI月增0.3%、年增2.6%、住房指數月
增0.4%，顯示能源雖是本次通膨回升主因 ，但住房及部
分服務價格仍具黏著性，令通膨降溫進程面臨反覆。
• 上游價格大幅攀升 ，成本傳導風險升高： 9月PPI最終需
求月增1.3%、年增6.3%，創2022年3月以來最大增幅，
其中最終需求商品月增 2.6%，能源價格大漲 10.8%，汽
油價格更跳升 20.3%；核心PPI月增0.5%、年增5.0%，
顯示價格壓力不僅來自能源 ，也涉及部分商品與服務環

節；後續是否進一步推升至 CPI，仍須觀察企業成本轉
嫁程度及需求變化。
• 市場看法： 通膨回升恐提高 Fed再度升息機率 ，並延長
高利率維持時間 ，甚至不排除進一步緊縮的可能 ，短期
有利支撐美債殖利率及美元表現 ，不過股、債市後續走
勢仍將受到貨幣政策預期與風險情緒變化影響 。資產配
置建議可關注具成本轉嫁能力 、現金流穩健的能源類股；
債券則宜以短中天期 、高信用評等標的為主 ，以降低利
率敏感度。黃金可作為分散地緣政治及通膨風險的輔助
配置，但不宜將任何單一資產視為必然受惠標的。
CPI 與核心 CPI 走勢變動

顧
供給面推動中國9月CPI升0.9%
• 供給面推動中國9月通膨復甦：中國9月通膨數據顯示，CPI年增率由8月的0.8%升至0.9%，連續兩個月擴大，但低於市場預期
的1.0%，物價上漲主要受到能源及旅遊價格帶動，而食品價格跌幅進一步擴大，使整體通膨壓力仍相對溫和，扣除食品與能源
後，核心CPI維持在1.0%，反映內需需求尚未明顯升溫 。另一方面，PPI年增率由3.8%大幅升至5.0%，優於市場預期，且月增
率達1.2%，創4月以來最大漲幅，顯示上游原物料及工業品價格回升，須關注未來是否逐步傳導至消費端價格。
• 展望後市： 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求 ，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力仍 在，彭博表示 以
GDP平減指數衡量，經歷三年下滑後，整體經濟預計將在2026年走出通縮，值得注意的是，這種供給面驅動的通膨復甦，將降

低官方推出需求刺激措施的緊迫感 ，而要反轉通縮循環正需要官方舉措，投資上短期可聚焦受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的
半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.14
通膨集中於能源和AI相關行業

顧
過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數
的兩倍
• 過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數的兩倍：主因為獲利帶動成長，整體而言，新興市場企
業獲利表現強勁 ，而且仍持續加速成長 ，高盛預估2026年新興市場企業盈餘成長率將達 55%，超過標普500企業盈餘成長速
度的兩倍，且新興市場股票估值較美國股市存在超過40%的折價空間，評價相對具吸引力，今年以來流入新興市場股市的資金
已接近500億美元，創下過去20年來最強勁紀錄之一。更重要的是新興市場國家目前貢獻了全球近 80%的AI硬體出口。隨著AI
產業持續發展，受惠範圍有望從少數龍頭企業擴大至更多國家及公司，特別是那些能夠協助解決供應鏈瓶頸的企業。
• 結論：後市來看在AI產業趨勢、相對低估的評價水準，以及強勁企業獲利成長三大因素支撐下，新興市場股票未來一年仍具備

進一步上漲的潛力，有機會延續目前的強勢表現。

AI投資支撐美國成長，融資需求升高使長端利率與信用風險並存
• AI基礎建設持續擴張 ，成為企業投資重要動能 ：大型雲端業者
持續投入資料中心 、半導體、網路設備與電力基礎設施 ，帶動
相關產業資本支出與供應鏈需求 。摩根大通資產管理指出 ，納
入資料中心開發商及半導體投資後，AI 基礎建設至 2030 年的
累計投資需求可能達約 5兆美元 ，顯示AI投資循環仍具規模 ，
但實際經濟貢獻與企業獲利 ，仍取決於投資效率及商業化成果 。
• 外部融資需求增加 ，債券供給與投資回報成關鍵： 隨AI投資規
模擴大，大型科技企業逐步增加公司債與外部融資 ，在企業債
與公債供給同步增加下 ，長天期利率面臨上行壓力 。若未來AI
獲利成長未能跟上資本支出擴張速度 ，可能壓縮自由現金流與

資本報酬率，進一步擴大信用利差 ，並提高高估值科技股的評
價修正風險
• 市場觀察：AI基礎建設仍是未來數年推動全球科技投資與企業
數位轉型的成長主軸，相關需求具備中長期發展空間，而2026
年市場已逐漸轉向檢視投資回報率 、現金流創造能力及融資結
構是否具備可持續性 ，因此單純依賴資本支出擴張已不足以支
撐所有AI概念股評價 。建議配置可兼顧具獲利與現金流支撐的
AI供應鏈，以及高信用評等 、中短天期債券；對長天期債券則
須評估供給與利率風險 ，並審慎檢視高負債 AI企業的償債能力
及投資回報。
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，永豐金證券， 2026.10.12

AI熱潮、高債務與網路威脅，IMF示警三大金融穩定風險升高
資料來源：Bloomberg Finance L.P， Dealogic，Zero day Clock and IMF staff calculations.， 2026.10.13
AI相關風險持續升溫 主權債券殖利率攀升 網路資安威脅升高
大型雲端科技企業債券發行規模
（占投資等級公司債市場比重) 10年期公債殖利率平均變動幅度 零時差漏洞占比（%）
• AI估值高度集中，市場重定價風險升溫：IMF於10月《全球金融穩定報告》指出，AI相關投資已成為全球成長的重要支撐，
但資金與漲勢高度集中於少數企業及市場，一旦獲利成長不如預期或估值遭重新檢視，可能引發科技股修正並透過風險偏好
下降擴散至整體金融市場。
• 通膨與財政疑慮恐推升債券市場波動： IMF認為，若能源價格推升通膨壓力或市場對財政永續性產生疑慮 ，主權債殖利率可

能進一步上升，進而提高全球融資成本與資產重估風險；在高利率與高債務環境下，金融條件快速緊縮的情況將明顯提高。
• 網路安全風險成為潛在威脅： 隨金融體系數位化程度提高 ，IMF將大型網路攻擊列為重要風險來源之一 。若攻擊事件波及支
付系統、金融基礎設施或雲端服務平台，不僅可能影響市場運作效率，也可能透過信心衝擊與流動性壓力擴大金融市場波動。
• 投資建議：市場成長動能仍受AI支撐，但在金融穩定風險上升情境下，建議採取「AI受惠股＋高品質債券＋防禦型資產」的
槓鈴策略；核心配置可聚焦全球AI基礎建設供應鏈及投資等級債，藉由企業長期獲利成長與債券避險功能，同時因應估值修
正及金融條件收緊風險。

AI隱憂浮現但動能仍在
• CoreWeave信用風險亮紅燈：市場憂心雲端業者在算力租賃業務產生足夠回報前，恐難以負擔龐大債務，導致信用風險升
溫。上週，AI雲端業者CoreWeave的5年期信用違約交換（CDS）利差一度突破1,140個基點，升至通常反映嚴重信用壓力
的水準；旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近15%，其中2032年到期的無擔保債券於上週三跌破面值的80%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延
至股票市場。
• AI半導體產業的基本面仍具韌性：全球最大半導體設備商艾司摩爾（ASML）表示，客戶已開始洽談2029至2030年的訂單；
晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期。與此同時，台灣出口增速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯
示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。

美銀美林調查指出，全球經理人對景氣與股市仍維持偏多立場
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
• 經理人樂觀情緒維持高檔： 美銀美林10月全球基金經理人調查顯示 ，市場風險偏好仍高 ，平均現金水位維持在 3.9%的低檔，
科技股配置則升至2020年8月以來最高，反映經理人對AI產業及企業獲利前景仍具信心 。然而，整體經濟成長預期已轉趨保
守，淨4%的受訪者預期未來 12個月經濟成長放緩 。極端風險方面，債券殖利率無序攀升仍居首位 ，占比31%；AI泡沫及通
膨再起分別為23%及20%。此外，39%的經理人認為，大型雲端業者資本支出是系統性信用事件的主要潛在來源 ，顯示市場
焦點已從AI需求成長延伸至龐大資本支出的獲利回收能力。

• 美國公債殖利率走高 ，債券的收益吸引力逐漸回升： 調查顯示，37%的受訪者表示，當美國10年期公債殖利率升至 6%時，
才會考慮由股票轉向債券；另有19%認為目前殖利率已具吸引力，顯示部分經理人已開始重新評估固定收益資產的配置價值 。
不過，債券整體仍處淨減碼，且經理人對科技股的配置持續升高 ，反映市場尚未全面轉向防禦。後續仍須關注美債殖利率變
化、AI資本支出的投資回報，以及企業獲利能否支撐偏高的市場評價。
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10

台積電財報佳卻未激勵股價，短線面臨總經干擾
• 台積電Q3營收、獲利及EPS同步改寫單季新高：營收年增50.9%、季增17.6%，稅後純益7,630.6億元，年增68.7％、季增8
％；每股稅後純益(EPS)29.43元，整體優於市場預期，但毛利率66.4％較第二季的67.7％下降。
• 財測展望：第四季財測營收預估 523~543億美元，季增上看15％、年增65％，毛利率預估65%~67%，反映高階晶片需求
依然強勁。全年展望方面，預期2026年美元營收年增約44%，較上季預估的「略高於40%」說法更加明確。此外，重申全
年資本支出600~640億美元不變。
• 市場反應：台積電法說會隔日股價小幅下跌，法說會未出現激勵行情，主要原因包含美國通膨升溫壓力、外資連續調節部位，

以及毛利率略為下降。短線籌碼面可能仍會調整，但台積電董事長魏哲家在法說會中強調，第二波AI需求正強勁興起，台積
電正透過多種途徑全力擴充產能，顯示高階晶片前景明確，中長期具投資價值，多家投資機構也紛紛上調目標價。
資料來源：台積電法說會，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.15。
法說會重點整理 說明
AI需求持續升溫
魏哲家坦言，目前仍難以判斷晶圓供需缺口將擴大或縮小，因為
客戶需求持續增加，台積電也正積極提高產能。儘管消費性電子
市場仍受到零組件漲價及總體經濟不確定性影響，但先進製程需
求依然強勁，公司預估2026年全年美元營收將年增約44％。
擴廠進度
(1)台南科學園區新建3奈米廠預計2027年下半年量產，並決定在
台南再增建一座3奈米廠，規畫2028年投入量產。
(2)美國亞利桑那州第二座晶圓廠已完成廠房興建，預計2027年
第三季量產，時程較原先規畫略為提前；日本第二座晶圓廠則將
導入3奈米製程，預計2028年量產。
台積電證實洽談Terafab
合作
魏哲家強調，目前相關合作仍在討論階段，尚未有結論。不過，

無論未來採取何種合作模式，晶圓廠都必須由台積電負責營運。
電力供應疑慮
美國電力供應情況是公司評估AI長期需求的重要因素之一，因此
台積電進一步確認當地電力供應狀況，目前相關研究及評估結果
均良好，尚未發現明顯問題。

• 全球經濟正處於「韌性仍在、風險升高」的階段：國際貨幣基金組織(IMF)指出，AI投資熱潮持續推動美國、台灣等國家成
長，但中東地緣政治衝突、高通膨風險、政府債務問題與AI泡沫化疑慮，也同時為市場埋下不確定因素。
• 市場永遠充滿雜音，定期定額紀律投資更具優勢：本許多人認為定期定額的核心在於「平均成本」，但更重要的是幫助投資
人克服人性。市場上漲時持續投入，可以參與企業獲利成長帶來的成果，市場下跌時持續扣款，則能以較低價格累積更多單
位數。換句話說，定期定額讓投資人不需要每天判斷：明天會不會跌？現在是不是太貴？要不要先停扣觀望？長期而言，紀

律投資往往比短期擇時的成效更佳。
• AI時代更適合定期定額：儘管IMF認為人工智慧發展為全球經濟成長帶來貢獻，但同時也警告，若市場對AI未來獲利能力過
度樂觀，不排除出現股市估值修正。根據歷史經驗，每一次市場重大修正往往也孕育下一輪成長契機，不論AI發展是否出現
泡沫，善用定期定額策略可以在機會與風險之間達到平衡。
韌性仍在、風險升溫，紀律投資更穩健
任一月份定期定額進場投資試算
註：以MSCI全球指數2010/1/31~2025/12/31試算。假設定期定額每月月底投入、
原幣計算。指數資料試算僅供參考，非投資商品之實際績效。資料來源Bloomberg。
短進短出錯失行情的代價大
註：以美國標普500指數為試算範例，期間為1980年~2025年。資料來源Bloomberg。

非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

息日前一日之淨值×100%。

績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

Document 046: 2026年10月LPR报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月LPR报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
2026 年 10 月 LPR 报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小 
 王青  冯琳 
2026 年 10 月 20 日，中国人民银行授权全国银行间同业拆借中心公布新版 LPR 报价：1
年期品种报 3.0%，上月为 3.0%；5 年期以上品种报 3.5%，上月为 3.5%。 
解读：

10 月两个期限品种的 LPR 报价保持不变，符合市场预期。主要是 9 月末央行调整完善若
干货币政策工具，主要政策利率保持稳定，这意味着 10 月 LPR 报价的定价基础没有变化。另
外，当前银行净息差仍然处在历史低位附近，报价行也缺乏主动下调 LPR 报价加点的动力。 
我们认为，近期 LPR 报价保持稳定，根本原因是三季度 GDP 增速回升到 4.5%，以高技术
制造业为代表的新质生产力领域加速发展。在此背景下，总量型货币政策保持定力，货币政
策加大逆周期调节力度优先选择结构性货币政策工具发力。 
展望未来，短期内 LPR 报价下调的可能性不大。主要原因在于，9 月 29 日央行调整完善
若干结构性货币政策工具，包括 PSL 利率下调 0.25 个百分点，支农支小再贷款额度上调
5000 亿，其中民企再贷款额度上调 3000 亿，科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度上调 2000 亿，
“报销”比例由原来的 60%上调至 100%；与此同时，财政部门也会同央行、金融监管总局，
首次出台了针对部分首套刚需购房贷款的贴息政策。我们判断，在以上结构性稳增长、稳楼
市措施出台后，接下来将进入一段政策效果观察期。短期内总量型降息政策跟进的可能性下
降，LPR 报价的定价基础会保持稳定。 
再往后看，综合考虑经济金融形势，预计 2027 年央行有望全面降息 10 到 20 个基点
（0.1 到0.2 个百分点），届时将引导 LPR 报价跟进下调。 
10 月 19 日央行宣布，“将研究择机把央行公开市场主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，
公开市场隔夜回购利率 将相应成为主要政策利率。”这意味着政策利率“换锚”的大方向已
经确定，未来 LPR 报价的定价基础将由现在的央行 7 天期逆回购利率切换为隔夜逆回购利
率。背后是隔夜政策利率与货币市场隔夜利率（ DR001)之间天然匹配，不存在期限利差，更
有利于提高货币政策传导效率。我们判断，切换时点可能已为时不远，不排除较快落地的可

### Page 2

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
能，届时央行将公开宣布隔夜逆回购操作利率。不过，切换过程带动 LPR 报价下调的可能性
不大；未来 LPR 报价变化，还会主要跟进政策利率水平本身调整。 
10 月 19 日央行还宣布，“推动贷款利率定价基准改革，从比较单一地参考贷款市场报
价利率（LPR）定价向更加多元的定价基准转型。”前期一些省份的银行已经开始发放以 DR
为定价基础的贷款。这意味着贷款利率市场化改革进一步推进，即从原来的单一 LPR 定价基
础，转为“LPR+DR”双锚。LPR 属于机构报价型利率，稳定性较高，而 DR 属于成交型利率，
市场化特征突出。信贷定价转为“ LPR+DR”双锚，意味着后期部分贷款利率市场化定价程度
会更高。这一方面有利于提升货币政策传导效率，加速政策利率向贷款利率传导，另一方面
也能更好满足不同市场主体的融资需求 ——对一些短期流动资金需求大、财务能力强的大型
企业来说，DR 基准贷款更能适配其信贷需求。我们判断，未来 10 年期国债收益率也可能成
为部分贷款的定价基础，背后是一些大型企业有条件在信贷市场和债券市场之间选择融资。
这意味着贷款定价基础多元化还将进一步推进。

For the month of October 2026, we estimate AirTAC’s monthly revenue will decrease

We expect October revenue will go down by 10% MoM to Rmb729mn/NT$3,447mn, 
mainly due to 19% fewer working days in Mainland China. However, we expect to see 
improvement in daily pneumatic revenue (Rmb32.3mn in Oct vs. Rmb 29.4mn in Sept) as 
the improvement in daily pneumatic revenue is driven by the strong growth from key 
customers, including electronics, followed by machine tools and battery, and we further 
expect demand to improve following the end of low season.

Earnings and TP revisions

(revise down 2026 revenue by 1%). We revise down our 2027E/2028E earnings estimate 
by 1%/1% to factor in a more conservative assumption on battery and linear guide 
business (revise down AirTAC revenue by 1%/1% in 2027E/2028E). We continue to see 
the company focusing on cost control as well as experiencing a better product mix 
outlook, but we expect some offset by rising material cost (revise down 2026E/2028E 
OPM by 0.1/0.1ppt).

Maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925), 
based on an unchanged target P/E of 26x on 2027 EPS.

Investment view: Accelerating monthly YoY from smaller segments could be a surprise. 
Maintain positive view on AirTAC 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we continue to believe the company is a key 
beneficiary of the factory automation/industrial automation localization trend in 
mainland China, and we expect the company will also benefit from the prolonged 
upcycle supported by the ongoing government stimulus policies. We see multiple key 
end-segments’ performance tracking ahead of the company’s 2026 full year guidance of 
20%+ YoY growth (see here), with battery/textile/machine tools/electronics delivering 
55%/46%/41%/23% YTD YoY growth while total revenue in Rmb is tracking at 23% YTD 
YoY growth. 
We continue to see solid monthly YoY growth from smaller segments, such as energy 
lighting and textile. However, the battery segment, which is the key growth driver in 
2025 (with 89% YoY in 2025), is seeing its first negative monthly YoY growth since Oct 
2024, while other major contribution sectors, such as electronics and automotive are 
seeing decelerating monthly YoY growth. As a result, we remain cautious on the 
potential of exaggerated orders (including advance or duplicate orders) despite new 
orders intake continuing to outpace shipments, and we will continue to monitor the 
underlying end-demand closely.

### Page 4

Maintain Buy on AirTAC with a lower 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925). 
Investment Thesis - AirTAC 
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share 
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality 
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast 
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner 
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC 
continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing 
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and 
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure 
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring 
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to 
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring. 
Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its 
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the 
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to 
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E 
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC. 
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on 
our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle). 
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected 
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in 
mainland China.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Alchip Technologies
Better allocation into 2027E; N2 project on track
Q3 sales up 243% QoQ on Trainium 3 ramp
Alchip reported September sales of NT$9,979 mn, up 14% MoM and 496% YoY,
concluding Q326 sales of NT$26,188 mn for +243% QoQ and 299% YoY, slightly
ahead of consensus, although below UBSe as we assumed a higher wafer allocation for
the Trainium 3 project, while sales contribution for its Chinese automotive customer and

NRE might also have came in lower than our expectation. We now expect Alchip's Q326
GM to arrive at 20.1%, down from 34.8% in Q226 on less favourable mix, leading to
OPM of 15.9%. We forecast Q326 EPS to grow 132% QoQ to NT$46.34.
Trainium 3  to sustain longer with potential upside; Trainium 4 tape-out on track
We expect Alchip's Trainium 3 monthly shipment run-rate to retain at current level into
H127F, and could extend into H227F with potential better allocation for Alchip with a

similar margin profile, subject to the final negotiation with its customer. We think
Trainium 3 deployments could stretch a little bit longer with a limited gap (vs prior ~3
months) before the next-generation Trainium 4 launch given the 3nm capacity tightness.
We continue to forecast overall Trainium 3 shipments at ~1.8mn units for 2026 and will
increase to ~2.8 mn units in 2027E, up 55% YoY, although Alchip's Trainium 3 shipment
could outgrow at 80%+ YoY given potential better allocation. For the Trainium 4 project

(2nm node), management noted it remains on track for tape-out before year-end and
still targets mass production by late-2027 or early 2028. Alchip continues to believe it
could enjoy a higher value-add given the increasing design complexity on chiplet and
higher wafer pricing for the 2nm node, while GM could retain at a similar level
compared with the current N3 project.
Engaging new customers for diversification
Alchip noted it remains in talks with another major CSP customer for potential adoption

of COT (customer-owned tooling) business model in the future for further cost saving.
We believe Alchip's competitive pricing,  strength for faster turnaround, and advanced
packaging knowledge put Alchip in a  good position to capture this opportunity, if the
customer decides to change the business model. Nevertheless, we do not expect
meaningful revenue contribution in 2027-28E from the new CSP customer. On the other
hand, we believe Alchip is in talks with other HPC customers for mid-size physical design

projects and this is likely to become a new revenue stream from 2027E. We estimate it
could book US$200-300mn revenue in 2027E from the new HPC customer.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with NT$6,600 price target
We lower our 2026E EPS by 11% primarily on lower Trainium 3 and automotive
contribution. We raise 2027E/28E EPS by 2%/5% to reflect  revenue contribution from
new HPC customer, offset by higher opex. We keep our PT unchanged at NT$6,600,
based on the same 30x 2027E EPS (vs. a 15-50x PE range). Stock is trading at ~20x 2027

P/E, below its peers and past five year average of 30x.

Company Description
Alchip was founded in 2002 by a group of Cadence managers with 20+ years of experience in
the semiconductor industry. It is an IC design and production service provider, and
management believes it can leverage third-party IP to enable customized chipset solutions for

customers cost effectively and fast to market. The company was funded by Cisco Investment
and Softbank Investment with VC funding in early 2000, then had its IPO on the emerging
board in Taiwan in 2010 and the main board in 2014.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Alchip using PE methodology.
Downside risks include slower-than-expected advanced node project diversification and
ramp, and growing competition in advanced projects from other IC design service companies.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.
Important Information About Sustainable Investing Strategies: Sustainable investing strategies aim to consider and incorporate environmental, social and governance
(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.
Incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing considerations may inhibit the portfolio manager’s ability to participate in certain investment opportunities that otherwise

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

Earnings upward revision on better OPM, robust
AI ASIC revenue on track; maintain Buy with TP
at NT$6,800
Mediatek will host an analyst meeting on Oct 30; we expect an inline GM/OPM
(guidance mid-point of 46%/15%), yet we expect a strong c30% QoQ growth
outlook for 4Q26 thanks to AI ASIC contribution and better mix/pricing for
consumer products. On the back of solid operating leverage, we are also looking for

OPM upside. We are looking for upbeat 4Q26 guidance.
With AI ASIC starts contributing in 4Q26 and multiplying growth into next two
years, we expect OPM expansion for Mediatek on the back of its operational
leverage. We lift our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /28 by 9%/ 25%/9%. We
maintain our Buy with TP$6,800 (30x of 2027 /28 EPS, from 35x previously, mainly
on recent macro concerns and concerns on AI capex beyond 2028).

### Page 7

Mediatek (2454.TW)
21 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 30x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.
Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst

expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient

valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of

information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and

investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.

Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.
Privately held companies:
The legal entities through which Citi conducts its business do not permit investment research recommendations, ratings,

not guaranteed. Private companies are generally not subject to reporting obligations, which may result in less publicly available
information. The information contained in this report may differ from information a private company may subsequently make
public or otherwise share with investors. Continuation of publishing private company reports or private company sections
within reports is at Citi's discretion, in accordance with internal policies.

The legal entities employing the authors of this report are listed below (and their regulators are listed further herein). Non-US
research analysts who have prepared this report (i.e., all research analysts listed below other than those identified as employed

may not be subject to the FINRA Rule 2241 restrictions on communications with a subject company, public appearances and
trading securities held by a research analyst account.

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

研究部對拓凱未來營運看法轉趨正向 ，主要是考量：(1)占營收比
重最大的自行車業務在 2H26 出現改善，隨著主力客戶新品銷售表
現較佳，帶動訂單增加，預期 2027 年自行車業務有望止跌回溫。
(2)航太、安全帽及其他類業務接單保持強勁，其中航太受惠於航
空座椅需求增加，安全帽則有高階產品及新客戶訂單貢獻，加上精
密塑件業務持續擴張 ， 將有助於優化產品組合 。(3) 2Q26 受子公司
一次性費用及匯率等因素干擾，毛利率與獲利下滑，但隨下半年出
貨規模擴大、產品組合改善，預期 2H26 獲利能力將逐步回升。研
究部預估拓凱 2026 年 EPS 13.52 元(-9%YOY)，2027 年EPS 15.28
元(+13%YOY)。因公司營運重回成長軌道，且產品組合改善有利於
中長期獲利表現，故以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎，給予 12 倍本益
比，目標價 183 元，投資評等由中立調升至買進。

重點摘要：

1. 主力客戶新車種上市，自行車業務回溫 ： 
拓凱營收約有 50% 來自碳纖維自行車車架與組件，最大核心
客戶為全球知名自行車 品牌-Specialized。雖然公司的自行
車業務在1H26衰退近二成，但受惠主力客戶 年中新品上市並
在終端市場銷售亮眼 ， 客戶對拓凱積極追加車架 、 輪圈與前叉
等複材組件訂單 ， 帶動拓凱近期自行車業務顯著改善 。 在去年
下半年低基期與今年新車拉貨潮帶動下，預計 2026全年自行
車營收減幅將收斂至個位數（研究部估減少8%YoY）。2027年
隨客戶新品需求與庫存去化完畢，預 估自行車業務營收將能
回溫，成長3%YoY。

2. 安全帽、航太及其他類業務展望正向，成長動能強勁： 
自行車以外業務為拓凱營運成長的核心動能 。 其中 ， 拓凱的安
全帽業務近年出貨增加 ， 且產品結構大幅改善 ， 高階產品占比
已達2/3，預計帶動今年安全帽營收大幅增長30%YoY，且因引
進電子智能安全帽新客戶，預期明年該業務營收仍可成長
7%YoY。球拍業務方面，今年出貨暢旺，預期今年球拍營收能
創下歷史新高 ， 全年度球拍營收預計成長18%YoY。 不過 ， 因近
兩年基期拉高，明年看法較謹慎，預計小幅衰退5%YoY。航太
醫療業務方面 ， 航太接單展望樂觀 ， 預期今年該類別營收增長
14%YoY，且1Q27起新增飛機座椅訂單將正式投產 ， 預計明年該
類別營收將再有12%YoY的增速 。 另外 ， 歸類在其他類之子公司

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

新鴻洲部分，受惠自行車煞把手、電子TOF精密塑件等產品出
貨增加 ， 營運動能依舊偏強 ， 預計該業務2026年、2027年營收
分別成長23%、13%YoY。

3. 一次性負面因素淡化，3Q26毛利率與獲利將回升： 
回顧拓凱2Q26財報 ， 受提列子公司一次性費用的衝擊 ， 導致單
季毛利率下滑至31.3%(季減3個百分點，年減6.7個百分點)，
加上業外有逾6千萬元的匯損，拖累季度獲利表現，2Q26稅後
淨利2.67億元(-19%QoQ，-5%YoY)，2Q26EPS下滑至2.94元。然
而 ， 此費用屬於非經常性的短期會計調整 ， 本業高階複材競爭
力並未受損 。 下半年隨自行車業務改善 ， 拓凱3Q26合併營收回
升至27.16億元(+6%QoQ，+12%YoY)。 此外 ， 由於一次性因素淡
化 ， 加上高階安全帽 、 航太及其他類產品出貨增加 ， 產品組合
優化，研究部預估拓凱3Q26毛利率將回升至33.8%，營業利益
5.41億元(+37%QoQ，+13%YoY)，稅後淨利3.55億元(+33%QoQ，
+1%YoY)，3Q26EPS 3.91元，獲利將較第2季明顯改善。

4. 2026年營收近百億元，預估2026年EPS 13.52元： 
受惠於安全帽 、 球拍 、 航太及其他業務成長 ， 抵銷自行車業務
的衰退 ， 預期拓凱全年營收可望接近百億元 ， 研究部預估拓凱
2026年合併營收99.73億元(+8%YoY)。不過，由於2Q26受一次
性費用及匯率等因素干擾，今年度毛利率預估降至33.1%，營
業利益16.91億元(-2%YoY)，稅後淨利12.28億元(-9%YoY)，
EPS 13.52元。

5. 各業務成長帶動獲利回升，預估2027年EPS 15.28元： 
展望2027年 ， 隨自行車業務恢復成長 ， 航太及安全帽新訂單持
續貢獻，以及其他類業務穩健擴張，研究部預估 2027年營收
104.34億元(+5%YoY)， 毛利率回升至33.8%， 營業利益18.50億
元(+9%YoY)，稅後淨利13.88億元(+13%YoY)，EPS 15.28元。

6. 越南產能持續擴充，並積極佈局新應用商機 ： 
因應客戶分散生產基地與供應鏈調整需求，拓凱持續擴充越
南複合材料產品線 ， 目前除自行車前叉 、 車架等零組件外 ， 亦
逐步增加航空座椅零組件的生產，未來航太板材及球拍等產
品亦有機會導入越南製造 。 公司也已著手規劃越南二期廠房 ，
強化海外生產彈性 。 此外 ， 中科后里新廠除作為營運總部及研
發據點外 ， 也將支援新業務開發 。 初期鎖定人形機器人關鍵的
腰部與膝蓋複材結構件與客戶進行共同開發，同時也積極切
入農業與商用無人機 、 低軌衛星等先端應用 。 子公司新鴻洲方

面 ， 則運用精密射出及模具技術 ， 計畫開拓半導體與人形機器

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com 
       廖婉婷 TINA_LIAO@uni-psg.com

臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元 ， 改寫單月新高，3Q26 營收 646.24 
億元，優於研究部原估的 607.09 億元，主因 AI 相關業務出貨
優於預期 ， 加上手機客戶進入新品旺季 ， 研究部據此上修 3Q26 稅
後 EPS 至 3.84 元（原估 3.47 元）。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺
季延續， 加上 GPU 與 ASIC 新一代主板開始放量 ，營收與獲利將
續創新高 。2027 年伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成
為主流， 營收可望各再增逾百億元；IC 載板亦受惠供給持續吃緊
及新產能陸續開出 ， 帶動 AI 相關營收占比突破三成 。 綜上所述 ，
研究部上修臻鼎 2026、2027 年稅後 EPS 至 12.11 元（+99.6% 
YoY，原估 11.81 元）及 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，原估 22.91 
元）。基於臻鼎產品橫跨軟板、類載板、HDI、HLC 與 IC 載板，
可為客戶提供一站式解決方案 ， 並提前掌握關鍵材料與設備產能 ，
在高階 PCB 供給吃緊下擴產得以大量開出搶奪市占，研究部維
持以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎及 28 倍本益比，目標價上調至 
730 元，維持買進投資評等。

重點摘要： 
1. 9 月營收改寫新高，上修 2H26 獲利預估 
臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元（+27.5% MoM、+36.1% YoY），改寫
單月新高 ， 其中伺服器 、 光模塊與 IC 載板合計營收年增逾三倍 ，
行動通訊亦呈雙位數月增 與年增 。3Q26 營收 646.24 億元
（+33.4% QoQ、+36.4% YoY），達研究部原估的 106.4%，明顯優
於預期 ， 主因 GPU 新一代主板進入量產 、1.6T 光模塊出貨放大 ，
加上手機客戶進入新品旺季。考量 AI 相關產品占比提升，研究
部預估 3Q26 毛利率為 25.5%（+2.4ppt QoQ、+3.5ppt YoY，原
估 24.7%），稅後 EPS 上修至 3.84 元（+81.9% QoQ 、+81.9% 
YoY，原估 3.47 元） 。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺季延續，加上 GPU 
與 ASIC 新一代主板放量，研究部預估 4Q26 營收 736.13 億元
（+13.9% QoQ、+29.4% YoY），稅後 EPS 4.75 元（+23.8% QoQ、
+70.4% YoY，原估 4.86 元），營收與獲利續創新高。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. AI 伺服器主板成為最主要成長動能 
AI 伺服器主板隨 GPU 與 ASIC 平台升級，晶片封裝尺寸、I/O 
數與傳輸速率同步提升，主板須在有限面積內完成更多高速訊號
的扇出與繞線。 傳統 HLC 以一次壓合搭配機械鑽孔與背鑽 ，層間
連接密度已近極限；高階 HDI 則以多次壓合與雷射盲孔逐層堆
疊，線路更細、訊號路徑更短，可降低高速傳輸損耗，帶動 GPU 
平台 OAM 等主板設計由 HLC 轉向 HDI。HDI 須投入大量雷射鑽
孔機與多次壓合產能，在設備與材料交期拉長下，全球高階  AI 
PCB 產能缺口約 15–20%， 訂單已排至 2028 年，2027–2028 年
產品報價亦將持續上調。臻鼎提早取得全球逾五成高階雷射鑽孔
機產能 ， 在建 22 座新廠中半數投入 HDI、 伺服器與光模塊產品 ，
在同業擴產受限下得以持續承接訂單，帶動伺服器 HDI 比重由 
1H26 的約五成於 2H26 明顯提升；繼 ASIC 專案今年大量出貨
後，GPU 平台新一代主板亦自 4Q26 起放量。研究部預估臻鼎伺
服器營收 2026、2027 年各增加逾 100 億元 ， 為未來兩年最主要
成長動能。

3. 臻鼎可望躍居 1.6T 光模塊 PCB 龍頭 
AI 伺服器傳輸速率由 800G 升級至 1.6T，單通道速率倍增使線
路須更細 、 阻抗控制更精準 ， 傳統減成法已難以達到 ， 光模塊 PCB 
因此改採 mSAP 製程。臻鼎過去為手機類載板主要供應商，具備 
mSAP 大量生產經驗 ； 在高階設備交期拉長 、 關鍵材料供給吃緊下 ，
臻鼎已掌握市場上多數高階設備產能並持續擴充，同時提前鎖定
上游材料，得以在淮安與泰國同步擴充光模塊產能，研究部推估
臻鼎可望在 2027 年 1.6T 世代成為全球光模塊 PCB 龍頭。隨 
1.6T 出貨占比由 2026 年的約五成提升至 2027 年逾七成，研
究部預估臻鼎光模塊營收由 2025 年約 9 億元躍升至 2026 年
逾 100 億元，2027 年再增至逾 250 億元。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. ABF 與 BT 同步吃緊，新產能與轉單推升載板營收 
AI 晶片封裝面積與層數持續放大，ABF 載板用量與製程難度同
步提升，惟新廠建置期長、上游材料吃緊，供需缺口持續擴大；
同業亦將部分 BT 產能轉往 ABF，使 BT 載板同步出現缺貨，兩
者報價自下半年起持續上漲。臻鼎 ABF 載板技術規格已達最大 
158×158mm、 最高 28 層 ， 深圳一廠 1Q26 轉盈並將於年底滿載 ，
二廠 1Q27 開始貢獻，第二園區 2028 年起約每年開出一座新
廠，且新產能幾乎全由國際客戶預先包下，高雄廠亦已量產先進
封裝相關載板。BT 載板雖無新建廠計畫， 但近期承接同業轉單並
調漲報價 ， 加上產品與客戶組合持續優化 ， 可望維持雙位數成長 。
隨 ABF 產能接續開出，研究部預估 ABF 占載板營收比重將由 
2025 年的 35% 提升至 2027 年約七成，IC 載板營收 2026、
2027 年分別成長 99.2% 及 89.0%。

表一、臻鼎三大高階應用產品時程

5. 高階新產能陸續開出，產品組合優化推升毛利率 
臻鼎 2026 年資本支出逾 800 億元，2027 年維持相當水準，廠
房數將由目前的 28 座擴增至 2030 年底的 59 座。新產能自 
2027 年起陸續開出：淮安 6 座新廠於 2026 年底完工後進入裝
機，HD 園區預計 3M27 投產、2Q27 大批量產；泰國新廠於 4Q26 
起試產，1H27 進入小量生產 ； 深圳第三園區與載板第二園區則預
計自 2028 年起接棒 （表二） 。新廠初期稼動率與良率仍在爬坡，
加上 2027 年折舊費用預計較 2026 年再增加 20% 至 30%，短
期將稀釋毛利率；惟新增產能以 AI 伺服器、光模塊與 IC 載板
為主，隨 AI 相關營收占比由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 2027 
年突破三成，研究部預估臻鼎毛利率將由 2026 年的 24.5% 提
升至 2027 年的 29.3%。

6. 上修 2026、2027 年獲利預估 
2026 年 AI 相關業務為主要成長動能 ，研究部預估伺服器 、光模
塊與其他營收成長 269.0%，IC 載板成長 99.2%，行動通訊受惠
摺疊機新品成長 6.9%，電腦消費則與 2025 年持平，AI 相關營
收占比將由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 25.9%。研究部預估 2026 
年營收 2,273.96 億元（+24.6% YoY，原估 2,267.70 億元），
毛利率由 24.1% 上修至 24.5%（+4.7ppt YoY ），稅後 EPS 由 
11.81 元上修至 12.11 元（+99.6% YoY）。 
展望 2027 年，伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成為
主流，營收合計成長逾一倍，IC 載板受惠 AI 客戶訂單放量、
ABF 比重提升及報價調漲，成長 89.0%，行動通訊與電腦消費則
分別維持中、低個位數成長。在高毛利產品占比提升、費用率隨
營收規模擴大回落，抵銷新廠折舊增加下，研究部預估 2027 年
營收 3,158.99 億元（+38.9% YoY，原估 3,083.20 億元），毛
利率提升至 29.3%（+4.8ppt YoY，原估 28.1%） ，營益率提升至 
17.9%（原估 14.8%） ， 稅後 EPS 上修至 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，
原估 22.91 元）。

評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上

中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%

矽力*-KY (6415 TT) 
資料中心與車載為成長主軸

永豐觀點 
矽力杰 3Q26 營收優於預期，4Q26 展望正向，雖短期毛利率較為波動，不過隨資
料中心與車載訂單加持，以及第四代製程平滲透率提升， 應有助於中長期毛利率回
穩。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，2027
年稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營運拓展，研究部
維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析 
矽力*-KY 企業永續評鑑整體為 B 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面
向平均得分分別為 B+等與 B 等。

### Page 2

2

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 2Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為四大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)36%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)35%、資訊產品(如筆電)9%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)6%、 車載14%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營收優於預期 ，4Q26 展望正向 ：矽力杰總計 3Q26 營收 72.83 億元
(+21%QoQ，+53%YoY)，落於研究部原預估 3Q26 營收達成率 102%(研究部原
預估季增 18%)，優於預期，主要係由資料中心為最主要推升力道，再者車載因新
款晶片推出與市占率提升而增 、 消費電子即便市場需求較為疲弱， 但因公司新款晶
片推出，故反而仍能向上。目前展望 4Q26 亦維持正向看法，營收變化主要仍需視
後段封測產能的供給程度而定。研究部預估矽力杰 3Q26 毛利率 47.63%，稅後獲
利 11.32 億元(-28%QoQ，+66%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.9 元，並預估 4Q26 營收 76.9
億元(+5.5%QoQ，+43%YoY)，毛利率 47.8%，稅後獲利 12.33 億元(+9%QoQ，
+53%YoY)，稅後 EPS 3.13 元。 
主要產能瓶頸在後段封測 ：目前矽力杰最主要的產能瓶頸在後段封測的支應，主要
是後段封測供給被 AI 應用排擠，目前除原有封測廠的產能擴充外，公司策略也持
續驗證新封測廠，以其紓解封測產能緊缺問題。至於晶圓產能，則矽力杰因具備第
四代 12 吋製程平台產能(第四代製程平台滲透率由 2025 年的個位數比重 ， 已將提
升至 4Q26 的 20%)，故反而晶圓較能充足的支應，明年第五代製程平台亦將加入
量產範圍。針對 2026 年毛利率走勢仍較有變數，係因影響毛利率因子正負交錯：
正向因子包括公司依據產品與客戶別而有不等的漲價措施 、 第四代製程平台挹注與
產品結構變化，負向因子則包括晶圓/封測成本上升、存貨依據庫齡政策而有損失
或回沖認列等影響。不過研究部認為隨 07/01 已開始進行漲價，理應能消弭部分
製造成本攀升的衝擊，遂預估 2H26 毛利率應能優於 2Q26 水準，中長期隨第四
代製程平台滲透率提升與產品結構變化， 理應有助於毛利率趨勢向上。 相較毛利率
是否獲得顯著提升，目前公司更關心在希冀產能能獲得更充足的支援， 以滿足客戶
的需求。 
資料中心與車載為後續成長主軸：資料中心、車載與工業將成為推升後續成長的主
軸：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營收比重 15%~16%，2027
年 目 前 維 持 預 期>20% 比 重 ， 公 司資 料 中 心 三 大 項 目 比 重 依 序 為光 通 訊
>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重較
低。公司過往以 400G 以下為主，今年 400G/800G 均已導入，隨市占率逐步提
升，又未來若主板核心電源出貨，更將進一步推動資料中心業務規模擴大。公司亦
表示 PSU 相關產品出貨亦隨伺服器需求而增，公司 PSU 產品包括 AC-DC 
controller/ESD/Boost/ Buck/Temp sensor等，目前針對 VPD 進展還沒那麼快。
(2) 車載營收比重可望由去年的 13%， 攀升至今年的15%~20%， 研究部認為車載
動力來自於公司車載晶片供應品項增加， 每台車的可供應的內含價值擴大， 並且客
戶群攀升，除中國品牌上量外，也進一步拓展海外市場。(3) 儲能與光伏需求，以
及充電樁應用，亦能拓展工業領域的需求。 
維持買進： 研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 258.35 億元(+37%YoY)，毛利率
47.65%，稅後獲利 47.05 億元(+90%YoY)，稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 341.9 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.44%，稅後獲利 60.39 億
元(+28%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營

營運現況與分析

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trưởng chậm 
nhưng biên lợi nhuận gộp vẫn được 
duy trì do tăng giá bán

Sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 chững lại 
do tâm lý nhập hàng thận trọng và 
hoạt động xây dựng phục hồi chậm

Biên lợi nhuận gộp được dự báo sẽ 
thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Tăng trưởng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất Hoạt động kinh doanh

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

Biểu đồ 3. Tương quan giữa tỷ lệ chi phí bán hàng/doanh 
thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

STOXX 600 Earnings Outlook provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

Analyst: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA                                           October 22, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 21.9% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 8.4%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 13.3% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 5.9%.

 47 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 55.3% reported results 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 58 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 60.3% reported revenue 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of October 26, 78 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

Through October 22, 47 companies in the STOXX 600 Index reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these, 55.3% reported 
earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

(since 2012) average surprise factor.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 is 21.9%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €160B in Q3 2026, compared to s hare-weighted earnings of €13 1.3B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Nine of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improv ement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, wh ile the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (134 .1%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €32.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €14.1B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-71.4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

Roots and routes –  
A framework for Global

September 2026

### Page 2

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  2

India’s financial coming of age has created a generation of investors at a 
distinctive crossroads: deeply connected to one of the world’s fastest -growing 
economies, yet increasingly aware that portfolios concentrated in a single market 
can accumulate risks over time. 
This report discusses the challenges faced by two distinct ‘Global Indian’ investor 
profiles. The first is the onshore Indian investor: a resident Indian whose wealth 
is largely denominated in domestic equities and fixed income, but who may still treat 
global markets as an occasional satellite allocation rather than a core portfolio 
component. The second is the offshore Indian investor : a non -resident Indian 
(NRI) based outside India with full access to global capital markets, yet who may 
be under-allocated to the growth story unfolding in their country of origin. Because 
global benchmarks understate India’s economic importance, investors seeking 
more meaningful participation in its growth story may consider allocating above 
benchmark levels, with NRIs’ potential stronger  affinity with India providing an 
additional reason to make this adjustment. 
For both groups, the starting point is the same: a concentrated bias towards either 
Indian or global markets can create risks that diversification can help mitigate 
without requiring investors to abandon their core convictions. For onshore Indian 
investors, global assets can provide structural currency diversification and access 
to sectors that are under -represented in Indian indices. Our Capital Market 
Assumptions (CMAs)  illustrate the potential portfolio benefits: increasing  
the allocation to global assets from 0% to 15% raises expected returns while 
reducing expected volatility meaningfully. As a result, the portfolio’s risk -adjusted 
returns improve. 
For offshore Indian investors, India offers a real yield premium, a rising weight in global indices and long-term growth that remains 
meaningfully under -represented in global benchmarks. A historical perspective illustrates the opportunity: since 2001,  
Indian equities have delivered over 10% annualised returns in USD terms, outperforming Developed Market equities over the 
same period. 
Onshore and offshore Indian investors may share the same connection to India, but they face very different portfolio challenges. 
There are key dividing lines that determine how each investor should think about diversification, risk management and the rol e 
of Indian assets within a broader investment portfolio.

Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Sylvain Huard 
Head of Asset Allocation 
Hannah Chew 
Portfolio Strategist

1 Two investor profiles, one shared 
opportunity

### Page 3

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  3

Base currency –  
the first and most 
important divide 
 The onshore Indian investor earns, spends, saves and ultimately retires in rupees (INR). Their 
relevant return is, therefore, an INR return. If the INR depreciates against the US dollar (USD), 
foreign assets rise in INR terms and can act as a natural hedge . If the INR appreciates, those 
same assets deliver lower INR returns than their local -currency performance would suggest. 
For the offshore investor, the currency problem is reversed. Their benchmark is typically USD-
based, and Indian markets are the exposure that introduces exchange-rate risk. A strong year 
for Indian equities in INR terms may look less compelling once translated back into USD at an 
unfavourable exchange rate.

Regulatory access –  
different constraints, 
different routes 
 The onshore Indian investor operates within the Liberalised Remittance Scheme (LRS) 
framework. Depending on their circumstances, this can offer meaningful or constrained access 
to global markets. Fund -of-funds and international exchange -traded funds (ETFs)  can also 
offer alternative access routes, as opposed to direct securities alone.  
For the offshore Indian investor, the challenge is less about access to India than choosing the 
most appropriate investment route. They can generally invest through onshore accounts, while 
offshore-listed funds and ETFs may offer a more convenient alternat ive. The preferred 
structure will depend on their residency, tax domicile and product eligibility, making careful 
planning important even where access itself is relatively broad. 
As an example, India’s Gujarat International Finance Tec -City (GIFT City), home to the 
country’s International Financial Services Centre (IFSC), is one potential implementation route 
available to both onshore and offshore Indian investors. For offshore investors, GIFT City offers 
an efficient way to access India-linked and global investment products with little or no need for 
INR conversion, while allowing foreign -currency repatriation. For onshore investors, it can 
facilitate USD -denominated exposure throu gh foreign -currency accounts under the LRS, 
providing a domestically regulated, yet globally oriented platform for portfolio diversification.

Portfolio risk –  
concentration on 
one side, under-
allocation on the 
other 
 The onshore Indian investor’s core risk is concentration and complacency: a portfolio almost 
entirely exposed to India’s domestic economic cycle, policy environment and sectoral 
composition. This is not a prediction that these will deteriorate, as India’s structural growth story 
remains compelling. Rather, this implies the risk is structural when all equity exposure sits 
within a single market, causing the portfolio to react simultaneously to the same domestic 
catalysts. By contrast, the offshore Indian investor faces the opposite problem: under-allocation 
to one of the world’s fastest-growing economies, driven partly by access frictions and partly by 
the difficulty of sizing a home-market allocation through a global investment framework.

### Page 4

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  4

A diversified portfolio makes sense 
regardless of the investor profile 
Before examining the specific assets available to Global 
Indian investors, it is useful to step back and ask a more 
fundamental question: why hold multiple asset classes at all? 
The simple answer is diversification. Because different asset 
classes play dif ferent roles and perform differently across 
economic and market conditions, combining them thoughtfully 
can reduce risk, create more consistent outcomes and build 
greater portfolio resilience than relying on any single asset 
class alone. 
Each major asset class tends to perform best under different 
economic conditions. Equities usually benefit when corporate 
earnings are growing and investor confidence is strong, but 
they can fall sharply when growth disappoints or credit 
conditions tighten. Bonds can provide income and may hold 
their value, or even appreciate, when growth slows and 
central banks ease policy, but they typically face pressure 
when inflation is high and interest rates rise. Gold has long 
been viewed as a geopolitical and infla tion hedge, and its 
relatively low correlation with equities and bonds can make it 
a useful portfolio diversifier. 
In practice, a portfolio concentrated in a single asset class – 
whether Indian equities, USD cash or any other standalone 
exposure – is implicitly relying on one set of economic and 
market conditions remaining favourable. A thoughtfully 
constructed multi-asset portfolio does not need to be the best 
performer in every year, but it is also unlikely to be the worst. 
Its purpose is to build resilience across market cycles: 
capturing growth when conditions are supportive, limiting 
downside when stress emerges, and compounding steadily 
over time in a way that no single asset class can consistently 
achieve on its own.

A practical asset-class toolkit for Global 
Indian investors 
Developed Market (DM) equities 
These are typically shares in large, established companies 
across the US, Europe, the UK and Japan and usually 
represent the largest and most liquid equity markets in the 
world. They are home to the technology giants, global 
healthcare leaders and consumer  brands that dominate their 
sectors globally – many of which have no equivalent in Indian 
indices. DM equities provide the broadest possible access to 
global corporate earnings growth, and for onshore investors, 
they provide USD and other major currency ex posure that 
structurally helps hedge against INR depreciation. For 
offshore investors, DM equities are the natural portfolio core, 
given their base currency and broad exposure of the asset 
class in global markets. 
Emerging Markets (EM) equities  
EM equities span a broad universe of developing economies, 
including China, South Korea, Taiwan, Brazil and other major 
EM markets. They offer greater growth potential than DM 
equities, but also tend to be more volatile. Their returns are 
supported by risi ng consumption, commodity cycles and the 
expansion of industry and the middle class. For Indian 
investors, EM equities provide exposure to regional peers that 
behave differently from Indian domestic markets, adding a 
layer of diversification within the equity sleeve.

1 The building blocks of a Global 
Indian portfolio

### Page 5

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  5

Indian equities  
Indian listed equities can be split up into two distinct sub-asset 
classes: 1) small - and mid -cap equities and 2) large -cap 
equities. The latter represent India’s established blue -chip 
companies with strong balance sheets and have relatively 
lower volatility within the Indian equity spectrum. These are 
typically the anchor of any Indian equity allocation. 
Meanwhile, small - and mid -cap equities tap into a different 
part of India’s growth story: the domestic consumption engine, 
the formalisation of the inform al economy and the 
entrepreneurial mid -market that scales rapidly in a high -
growth environment. They offer meaningfully higher potential 
returns, but also comes with higher volatility and lower 
liquidity, making the appropriate allocation dependent on the 
investor’s risk tolerance and investment time horizon.  
DM Investment Grade bonds 
Developed market investment grade bonds encompass two 
closely related asset classes that play a core defensive role in 
portfolios. Government bonds, such as US Treasuries, 
German Bunds, and UK Gilts, are issued by highly rated 
sovereigns and are widely regarded as the closest equivalent 
to a risk -free asset within global markets. Investment grade 
corporate bonds, issued by financially strong companies rated 
BBB- or above, offer higher yields than government bonds in 
return for accepting a modest amount of credit risk. 
Together, these assets provide an important source of portfolio 
stability. Government bonds have historically performed well 
during periods of economic uncertainty and market turbulence, 
helping to cushion equity losses when investors seek safer 
assets. In vestment grade corporate bonds enhance overall 
income generation while maintaining relatively low volatility, 
allowing investors to improve return potential without taking on 
the higher risks typically associated with equities. 
EM USD bonds 
EM USD bonds are USD -denominated debt issued by EM 
sovereign and quasi-sovereign borrowers, including countries 
such as Indonesia, Mexico, Brazil and South Africa. Their 
USD denomination removes direct exposure to the issuers’ 
local currencies, making them  a more accessible entry point 
into EM fixed income than local -currency bonds, particularly 
for offshore investors with hard -currency liabilities. For 
onshore investors, EM USD bonds add hard -currency fixed-
income exposure and can diversify portfolios conc entrated in 
Indian equities and INR interest -rate risk. However, their 
returns still depend on global interest rates, credit spreads and 
investor risk appetite. 
India government bonds  
The Indian bond market offers something that is increasingly 
rare globally: a genuine positive real (net -of-inflation) yield. 
India’s 10-year government bond yielded around 6.7% in mid-
2026 against a headline inflation of about 4.4%.  This 
represents a positive real yield of nearly 2.8% that compares 
favourably to most DM alternatives. India’s inclusion in global 
bond indices is now also channelling systematic passive 
inflows into this market, providing an additional structural 
demand tailwind.  
Gold 
Gold occupies a unique role in this framework particularly for 
Indian investors. Beyond its universal function as an inflation 
and geopolitical hedge, gold in INR terms has historically 
delivered strong returns during periods of INR weakness 
because gold prices are globally denominated in USD. When 
the INR depreciates, the INR value of gold rises, providing a 
natural cushion against the same currency dynamics that 
erode domestic purchasing power.

The case for global exposure for onshore Indian investors  – Most resident 
Indian investors already diversify across asset classes, typically holding a mix of 
equities, fixed income and, in some cases, commodities such as gold. However, 
this diversification is usually implemented through domestic assets. The gap, 
therefore, is not necessarily a lack of asset-class breadth, but a lack of geographic 
diversification. This is where global investing can play an important role: by adding 
exposure to different economies, currencies, sectors and market cycles  
beyond India.  
Argument

Since India’s market liberalisation in the early 1990s, the INR has depreciated significantly 
against the USD, weakening from approximately INR 31 per USD in 1994 to more than INR 95 
per USD by August 2026 – an annualised depreciation rate of 3.4%. This long-term depreciation 
is not simply a market fluctuation; it reflects a structural feature of an economy that has 
persistently run higher inflation than the US. For investors holding only INR-denominated assets, 
this creates a gradual erosion of global purchasing power over time. USD-denominated foreign 
assets can, therefore, serve as a structural currency hedge, helping preserve international 
purchasing power rather than representing a speculative currency bet. 
The INR 
depreciates 
structurally

1 Why an India-only portfolio may not

### Page 7

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  7

Argument

Indian equity indices remain heavily weighted towards the financial, consumer discretionary and 
industrial sectors. By contrast, technology – the largest sector in global DM indices – has a much 
smaller representation in India’s listed market, despite the scale and importance of India’s 
broader technology economy. This gap has become more significant with the rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI). While India has strong technology talent and growing AI adoption, Indian 
markets offer little -to-no exposure to technology in the investible equity market. As a result, 
investors with portfolios concentrated in Indian equities have limited exposure to global 
technology leaders. Adding DM equities, therefore, provides sector diversification, including 
greater partici pation in the growth opportunities associated with AI, as well as geographic 
diversification.

markets

Argument

Indian equities respond sharply to domestic catalysts: RBI rate decisions, monsoon outcomes, 
fiscal borrowing announcements and India -specific political developments. When these factors 
turn negative, they tend to affect domestic equity positions simultane ously. Global equity 
markets are not driven by the same catalysts, so a domestic Indian growth slowdown does not 
cause US corporate earnings to fall or other global equities to retreat. Adding a global equity 
sleeve means that not all equity exposure moves in tandem during a domestic downturn.  
The same logic applies to bonds. Indian bonds respond primarily to RBI monetary policy, 
domestic inflation and India’s fiscal borrowing calendar. These drivers are largely independent 
of the global rate cycle. An investor holding only Indian equities and Indian bonds has all of their 
assets exposed to the same set of domestic macro factors at once. A portfolio that blends Indian 
and global assets reduces this localised cycle dependency, spreading exposure across markets 
that follow different cycles. 
Market cycles are 
not synchronised

### Page 9

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  9

The case for Indian asset exposure for offshore Indian investors  – India 
stands out as one of the few major economies where 6 -7% annual real GDP 
growth remains a credible base case through the end of the decade. For offshore 
investors, the allocation case is not built on growth alone, but on four reinforcing 
pillars: a structural growth premium unmatched among large economies; reforms 
that have improved the quality and breadth of that growth; a demographic 
dividend that still has years to run; and a digital public infrastructure revolution 
that is lifting productivity at population scale.

The growth premium  
According to the International Monetary Fund, India remains the world’s fastest-growing major economy. Real 
GDP expanded by 7.6% in 2025 and is projected to grow by 6.5% in 2026 – outpacing China at 4.4%, G7 
economies at 1.6%, and EM and developing economies at 3.9%. This is not a one-year outlier. Apart from the 
Covid-19 pandemic shock, India has sustained a 6 -8% real GDP growth range for much of the past decade, 
reinforcing its structural growth premium. 
This structural growth is also reflected in equity -market performance. Since 2001, Indian equities have 
delivered annualised returns of 10 .1% in USD terms , outperforming DM equities, which returned 7.5% 
annualised over the same period. This long-term track record reinforces the case for including Indian equities 
as a deliberate source of growth within an offshore investor’s portfolio. 
For offshore investors, there is a clear benchmark gap. A global equity benchmark allocation of around 1.3% 
to India materially understates the country’s economic weight. India accounts for roughly 8.2% of global GDP 
on a purchasing power parity basis in 2025, which is significantly higher than its equity index weight. As Indian 
capital markets deepen and foreign ownership constraints ease, this gap between economic weight and index 
representation should continue to narrow, while rising benchmark inclusion can create a recurring source of 
passive inflows into Indian assets.

Economic and market reforms 
India's growth story over the past decade has not just been about the pace of GDP expansion. It also reflects 
a series of market -oriented reforms that have strengthened the investment environment and made growth 
more durable and accessible to long -term cap ital. Examples include the Goods and Services Tax, the 
Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, the Real Estate Regulation and Development Act and measures to deepen 
and formalise domestic capital markets. Taken together, these reforms have improved the structural 
competitiveness of the Indian economy in ways that show up in productivity growth, foreign direct investment 
inflows and market depth – not just in headline GDP numbers.

1 Why Indian assets still belong in every 
offshore Indian investor portfolio

### Page 10

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  10

Demographic dividend  
India’s demographic advantage is a long -term structural tailwind, not a short -term population bulge. The 
country has the world’s largest working -age population and, with a median age of around 29.2 (according to 
Worldometer’s latest data), remains one of the youngest major economies. As more Indians enter the formal 
workforce, earn higher incomes and build financial savings, they can support a broader domestic consumption 
base, deeper financial intermediation and continued cap ital-market growth. The shift in household savings 
from physical assets, such as gold and real estate, towards financial assets reinforces this trend. Monthly 
systematic investment plan (SIP) inflows into mutual funds, which have risen from approximately INR 36.6bn 
in 2016 to more than IN R 310bn in 2026, are a clear expression of this demographic and savings transition. 
They also create a durable, domestically -sourced demand base for Indian equities that is less dependent on 
foreign capital flows. 
However, the demographic dividend is not automatic; its realisation will depend on equipping a growing 
workforce with employable, industry -relevant skills and creating sufficient productive jobs. India has made 
meaningful investments in this direction thro ugh the Skill India Mission, including vocational training, 
apprenticeships and courses aligned with emerging sectors. Sustained improvements in training quality, 
employer alignment and job creation will, therefore, be critical to convert India’s demographic scale into lasting 
gains in productivity, income and economic growth.

Digital infrastructure 
India's digital public infrastructure – collectively known as the India Stack – has built a low-cost, inter-operable 
rails system that is accelerating formalisation, financial inclusion and commercial productivity at a speed with 
few global parallels. Exam ples include Aadhaar for identity, the Unified Payments Interface for payments, 
DigiLocker for documentation and the Open Network for Digital Commerce. Together, these platforms are 
productivity infrastructure: reducing the cost of doing business, formalising the informal economy and 
expanding the addressable market for financial services companies, fintechs, consumer platforms and logistics 
businesses in ways that compound into earnings growth over time.

The India fixed income opportunity 
India's 10-year government bond yielded around 6.7% in mid -2026, against headline consumer inflation of 
approximately 4.4% – implying a real yield of around 2.4%. This compares to near-zero or negative real yields 
in much of the developed world over the past decade and to real yields of 1-2% available in most comparable 
EM bond markets.

### Page 11

For onshore Indian investors, adding global assets can improve portfolio 
resilience without diluting expected INR returns. Our modelling shows that moving 
from a fully domestic portfolio to a 15% global allocation lifts the expected return 
only marginally,  from 8.42% to 8.44%, but reduces expected volatility more 
meaningfully, from 18.38% to 16.86%. This improves the risk-adjusted return from 
0.46 to 0.50. The benefit comes from exposure to markets, sectors and currencies 
that do not move in lockstep with I ndian assets, helping reduce domestic 
concentration risk while adding access to different structural growth drivers.

### Page 12

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  12

For offshore investors with globally diversified portfolios, Indian assets are best viewed as a deliberate 
strategic tilt towards a differentiated source of long -term growth, rather than as a near -term portfolio 
efficiency trade. Based on our forward -looking Capital Market Assumptions (CMAs), increasing the 
allocation to Indian assets to 15% leaves the expected USD return broadly unchanged at 6.10%, while 
expected volatility rises from 10.32% to 11.00%, reflecting the additional EM and currency risks.  
However, these aggregate estimates may not fully capture the opportunity in Indian financial market assets. 
Our CMAs show that the case for Indian equities is compelling when viewed from a returns perspective 
alone. 
The challenge to portfolio efficiency arises from Indian equities’ relatively high expected volatility. For 
context, our CMAs suggest an expected volatility of 29% for Indian large -cap equities. This number is 
considerably higher than the level experienced over the last 10 years (18.9%) and modestly higher than the 
level over the past several decades. This is a key point because the drag on risk-adjusted returns in Fig. 8 
comes from the higher expected volatility of Indian equities rather than any challenge to the expected return. 
As an example, Fig. 9 illustrates that calibrating Indian equity volatility to more recent market experience 
produces a more balanced assessment of portfolio risk and return. Using recent history as an example 
shows that risk-adjusted returns actually improve modestly from 0.79 to 0.81.  
Overall, this means the debate is less about how high a return Indian equities are expected to deliver over 
the coming years. Instead, how volatile Indian assets are likely to be is key to whether adding them to global 
portfolios improves risk-adjusted returns.

### Page 13

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026 13
Pulling this together, we would argue that a strong case exists for a measured allocation to Indian assets –
particularly equities – exists, in our view, provided two conditions are met.
First, investors should retain conviction that the structural drivers of India’s return premium – including
earnings growth, improving governance and an expanding investable universe – remain intact. This is key

to being comfortable with the expected return of Indian equities and bonds.
Second, investors must be willing and able to tolerate the volatility associated with Indian equities. While
one could argue that the assumed volatility in our CMAs is high relative to the level experienced in recent
decades, under most scenarios, it would remain meaningfully higher than that of DM equities.
Taken together, offshore Indian investors should consider incorporating Indian assets within a globally

diversified portfolio. However, the allocation should be deliberate and sized in line with their objectives and
risk tolerance, reflecting a conscious investment view and not just the familiarity of a home bias.

CMAs.
These results show that cross-border diversification serves different needs for different investors but leads
to the same portfolio conclusion. For onshore Indian investors, global assets can help lower volatility,

broaden sector exposure and provide a structural hedge against INR depreciation. For offshore Indian
investors, India offers a deliberate growth and real -yield allocation that is under -represented in global
benchmarks. In both cases, combining Indian and global assets can build a more resil ient portfolio across
market cycles.

### Page 14

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  14

Resident onshore Indian investors and offshore Indian investors begin from opposite starting points. One 
is anchored in India and looking outward; the other is anchored globally and looking inward. Their regulatory 
regimes, base currencies and available instruments differ. Yet the portfolio destination is the same: 
meaningful exposure to both India and the world. 
That conclusion reflects the reality of an interconnected investment world. Indian corporate earnings are 
shaped by global commodity prices, technology supply chains and demand from DMs. At the same time, 
companies in the US, Europe and Japan are exposed to the consumption growth of emerging economies, 
including India. Bond markets may respond first to domestic central banks, but they are still influenced by 
global rate cycles; gold is priced in USD, yet its impact is felt across currencies. No investor can  afford to 
treat the other half of the world as irrelevant. 
What differs is not the principle, but the path. The onshore Indian investor adds global exposure as a 
deliberate extension of an India -centric portfolio, seeking additional return opportunities while gaining a 
structural hedge against INR depreciation. Th e offshore investor builds India exposure as a deliberate 
overweight above global benchmarks, accepting INR volatility as the cost of accessing India’s growth 
premium and real-yield opportunity. The appropriate allocation will differ by profile and risk appetite, but the 
core message is the same: both investors need both markets.

destination

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 3.0% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.5% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.4% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

《醫療器械喜憂參半，但必要性手術需求仍佳》
近月醫療服務設備股為健護中股價表現較差 ，主因：
1)亞培受到產品責任險達成部分和解協議 ，後續還
有其他聯邦 、州判決仍為股價壓力； 2)波士頓科學
受資安事件影響生產宣告無法達成財測 ，HRS的左

心耳封堵術 (LAAC) 指南適用 WATCHMAN、 亞培
Amulet，可同時做消融+LAAC，但LAAC不能完全
替代口服抗凝血藥物 (OAC)對預防中風的效用 ，仍
為二線，等同喜憂參半 。另受自付成本增加 ，預期
擇期、外科手術需求疲軟延伸至年底 ，機器人手術
成長率區間接近 14.5%，下半年低於上半年 ，將以
擴大翻新系統的布署降低門檻及增加門診手術需求 ，
擴大目標市場 ，但必要醫療手術需求健康 ，心血管
類手術下半年仍成長，醫療器材基本面喜憂參半。
《星級獎金與醫療成本控管力使長期獲利分化》
每月聯邦醫療保險優勢計畫投保人數成長趨勢持續 ，
CMS預計於2026年10月中公布 2027年醫療優勢計
畫(MA)星級評等，因評級變嚴將影響醫保公司會員
留存率、競爭力及2028年品質獎金收入不確定性增
加，低於4星級業者面臨品質獎金減少與長期獲利承
壓風險。另無意外法案獨立爭議解決 (IDR)仲裁成本
持續推升商業保險理賠支出 ，增加2027年雇主團體
及ACA市場保費定價壓力 。管理式醫療業者今年展

現利潤優先於市占率、砍不賺錢計畫/人數拉抬利潤
率，但明年若保費調漲幅度不及醫療成本增速 ，恐
推升醫療損失率 、壓縮承保利潤 。後續獲利關鍵在
於星級評等 、保費調整能力及理賠成本控管 ，業者
間的長期獲利表現可能進一步分化。
